        "markdown": "[Skip to main content](https://www.hotels.com/go/south-korea/information-about-busan#main_content)\n\n![Busan Information](https://cdn.sanity.io/images/nxpteyfv/goguides/d2989668a84464d5d88e52e38a11e2282e630de2-1600x1066.jpg?fp-x=0.5&fp-y=0.5&w=1600&q=75&fit=max&auto=format)\n\n# Busan Travel Essentials\n\n## Useful Information to Help You Start Your Trip to Busan\n\nContent\n\n1\\. When is the best time to travel to Busan?\n\n2\\. Busan basics\n\n3\\. How to get to my hotel in Busan?\n\n4\\. How to get around Busan?\n\n5\\. What are the main annual events in Busan?\n\n6\\. What are the main landmarks in Busan?\n\nOur Busan information guide comprises ways of getting to and around in South Korea’s bustling port city. The city has had quite a vivid past, with major attractions ranging from hot springs and waterfalls to nature reserves. The city is also famous for its yearly international film festival, attracting renowned celebrities from all over the world.\n\nFerries and boats from Japan travel to and from [Busan](https://www.hotels.com/go/south-korea/busan) regularly, together with flights from countries such as Thailand and the Philippines. Like most cities in South Korea, Busan is also served by an efficient public transportation system.\n\n[Busan Hotels](https://www.hotels.com/de1639028/hotels-busan-south-korea/)\n\n### 1\\. When is the best time to travel to Busan?\n\n![](<Base64-Image-Removed>)\n\n1. Located at the southeastern tip of South Korea, Busan has a humid subtropical climate. Extreme temperatures are rare with an all-year-round average temperature of about 15°C.\n2. The warmest month in Busan is August with an average temperature of 26°C, while January is the coldest month with a daily average of 3°C.\n3. Precipitation ranges around 6 days per month from October to February. Rainy days increase between March and June with an average of 9 wet days per month, reaching a peak in the July–September period with up to 13 rainy days per month. Typhoons can occur in late summer and occasional snowfalls can be expected from December to March.\n4. Summer (July–August) is the peak season in Busan. The crowds, heat and humidity, as well as higher accommodations rates, make the city less attractive for travel at this time of the year, though.\n5. The best times to visit Busan are in spring and autumn. The May–June and September–November periods bring decent weather and more affordable hotel prices than in summer.\n\n### 2\\. Busan basics\n\n![](<Base64-Image-Removed>)\n\n1. **Plug type:** C and F\n2. **Electricity:** 220 V – 60 Hz\n3. **Currency:** South Korean won (₩)\n4. **International dialing code:** +82 51\n5. **Emergency telephone number:** 119 (fire and medical emergency), 112 (police)\n\n### See also\n\n[![Best Family Spots in Busan](https://cdn.sanity.io/images/nxpteyfv/media-studio/4eac2877cf1372bc2ccd04c48bdf8344d6cdc1a9-330x312.png?fit=max&w=250&h=250&q=75&auto=format)\\\\\n\\\\\n9 Best Things to Do with Kids in Busan\\\\\n\\\\\nSouth Korea](https://www.hotels.com/go/south-korea/kr-best-busan-family-things-to-do)\n\n[![Franchise Card Image](https://cdn.sanity.io/images/nxpteyfv/media-studio/4eac2877cf1372bc2ccd04c48bdf8344d6cdc1a9-330x312.png?fit=max&w=250&h=250&q=75&auto=format)\\\\\n\\\\\n10 Best Festivals in Busan\\\\\n\\\\\nSouth Korea](https://www.hotels.com/go/south-korea/kr-best-festivals-busan)\n\n[![Best Night Spots in Busan](https://cdn.sanity.io/images/nxpteyfv/media-studio/4eac2877cf1372bc2ccd04c48bdf8344d6cdc1a9-330x312.png?fit=max&w=250&h=250&q=75&auto=format)\\\\\n\\\\\n10 Best Things to Do After Dinner in Busan\\\\\n\\\\\nSouth Korea](https://www.hotels.com/go/south-korea/kr-best-busan-nightlife)\n\n### 3\\. How to get to my hotel in Busan?\n\n![](<Base64-Image-Removed>)\n\n### Gimhae International Airport (PUS)\n\nTraveling to Busan by air is easy as over 20 international airlines offer regular flights to and from Gimhae Airport. The airport also serves many cities within South Korea and is well connected to Seoul by air. It’s 10 km west of Busan as the crow flies and is roughly a 15-minute drive from the airport to downtown.\n\nFree Wi-Fi is available throughout the airport. Ground transportation options available are as follows:\n\n1. Taxi cab stands are across the road from Gate 3 of the international terminal and across the road from Gate 4 of the domestic terminal.\n2. The Light Rail Transit airport station is 30 meters to the right across Gate 3 of the international terminal and across Gate 4 of the domestic terminal.\n3. Intercity bus stops can be found in front of both the domestic and international terminals, next to the taxi stands.\n4. Automobile rental agencies are found on the 1st floor of the domestic terminal.\n\n### Ferries and Boats to Busan\n\nBusan is one of the most famous seaports in South Korea – regular ferry services run from international locations such as Osaka and Kyushu Island. The most economical ferry service is Kanpu Ferry – these overnight ferries ply the waters between Busan and Shimonoseki.\n\nBoats travel between Busan and Kyushu Island and local ferries run between Busan and Jeju about 3 times a week. A oneway ferry trip to Busan from Jeju takes about 12 hours.\n\n### Busan Trains\n\nBusan has several railroad stations – the ultramodern Busan Station is located between Nampodong and Seomyeon. Subway Line 1 is easily accessible and there are several homestays and hotels in and around this station.\n\nAnother station near Subway Line 3 is Gupo station, right next to the Gupo subway stop. It’s one of the more convenient stations to catch a train in Busan. There are other benefits of boarding a train from Gupo station – tickets to Seoul are cheaper and the station is comparatively less crowded than most Busan stations. The only downside to boarding a train from this station is its distance from the main city.\n\nKTX is one of the most popular railroad companies in Busan. It offers services between Busan and Seoul. Tickets are dispensed from automated vending machines.\n\n### Buses in Busan\n\nBusan has 2 major bus stations – Dongbu Intercity Bus Terminal and Seobu Intercity Bus Terminal – and several smaller bus stops. Almost every major city or county in South Korea has regular bus services to Busan.\n\n### 4\\. How to get around Busan?\n\n![](<Base64-Image-Removed>)\n\n### Travel tips\n\nBusan is too large of a city to be explored on foot – apart from short distances – and riding a bike can be somehow hazardous due to the freestyle way of driving of many people in South Korea. Fortunately, the city hosts an extensive and very convenient public transportation system featuring subways and bus lines. Slightly more expensive, taxi cabs are also an effective way of getting around.\n\n### Transportation cards in Busan\n\nUsing a transportation card is the easiest way of getting around Busan by public transportation and taxi cabs. There are 3: Cashbee, Mybi, and Hanaro. Cashbee Card is the most widely used. Using the card saves you time and money, as well as entitles you to discounts on subway and bus tickets. Note that if you already purchased a T-money Card in Seoul, it also can be used in Busan.\n\nBusan transportation cards are available in convenience stores – 7-Eleven, Ministop, GS25 – and from vending machines in metro stations.\n\n### Traveling on the subway in Busan\n\nThe subway is one of the most widely used for commuting within Busan. There are 5 lines, as follows:\n\n1. Line 1 (orange): Dadaepo Beach–Nopo-dong\n2. Line 2 (green): Yangsan–Jangsan\n3. Line 3 (brown): Daejeo–Suyeong\n4. Line 4 (blue): Minam–Anpyeong\n5. Busan-Gimhae Light Rail Transit (purple): Sasang–Kaya University\n\nThey connect all the bus stations and major city hot spots. Most of the signages and public announcements are in Korean, Chinese, Japanese, and English, making it easy for foreigners to get around the city by subway.\n\nThere are automatic ticket vending machines, where you can buy your ticket. One-day passes are available if you foresee quite a lot of subway travel on that day and if you do not have a transportation card.\n\n### Traveling by bus in Busan\n\nLike the subway system, buses in Busan have information written in English at bus stops. Aboard the bus, the announcement of the next destination is also done in English and Korean. Fares can be paid in cash – the exact change required – or with a prepaid transportation card. You exit the bus from the rear door.\n\n### Taking a taxi cab in Busan\n\nTaxi cabs in Busan are available just about everywhere in the city. There are 3 main types of taxi cabs, all of which are metered with fares determined by distance and time. Virtually all taxi cabs operating in Busan accept cash, credit cards, and prepaid transportation cards. A late-night surcharge of 20% applies to all taxi cabs.\n\n1. **Regular Taxi cab** The cheapest taxi cabs in Busan are the regular or standard taxi cabs, which are colored gray. They can be easily flagged down on the streets. As most taxi cab drivers do not speak English, having your destination address written in Korean on a piece of paper is a good idea.\n2. **Deluxe Taxi cab** Deluxe taxi cabs are often nearby major hotels and popular tourist spots. Fares are usually around 40% higher compared to regular taxi cabs. The plus side of these vehicles are English-speaking drivers, more spacious seats and safer service. To identify a deluxe taxi cab in Busan, look out for vehicles in black with yellow stripes and a sign on top.\n\n### Renting a automobile in Busan\n\nUnless you plan long trips outside the city, renting a automobile is not a recommended option for getting around Busan. Anyway, if you decide to make this choice, you’ll need to be 21 years old and in possession of an international drivers license in addition to your foreign drivers license of over 1 year.\n\n### Riding a bicycle in Busan\n\nDespite the hazards mentioned above and the hilly terrain, riding a bike can be a pleasant option on clear days, especially when exploring the many ecological parks in Busan. Bike rental shops can be found at most parks around the city, including Samnak Ecological Park, Daejeo Park, and Hwamyeong Park, among others.\n\n### 5\\. What are the main annual events in Busan?\n\n![](<Base64-Image-Removed>)\n\n### Haeundae Sand Festival\n\n1. **What:** This spectacular event gathers some of the best sand sculptors in the world alongside a fringe festival with art exhibitions and other festive happenings.\n2. **When:** Late May\n3. **Where:** Haeundae Beach\n\n### Busan International Rock Festival\n\n1. **What:** Held since 1999, Busan International Rock Festival presents Korean and international artists including up-and-coming bands playing Indie, rock, and metal. It’s one of the longest-running music festivals in South Korea.\n2. **When:** Late July\n3. **Where:** Samnak Ecological Park\n\n### Busan International Film Festival\n\n1. **What:** Busan International Film Festival was founded in 1996 and has become one of the most notable film festivals in Asia. Watching new movies from all around the world and enjoying potential interaction with famous filmmakers and actors are part of the annual event.\n2. **When:** October\n3. **Where:** Busan Cinema Center and BIFF Square\n\n### 6\\. What are the main landmarks in Busan?\n\n![](<Base64-Image-Removed>)\n\n### Haedong Yonggungsa Temple\n\nThis Buddhist temple on the northeastern end of Busan was built in 1376 and is one of few temples in Korea built on the seaside. It has dozens of sculptures and statues and is famous for its Hakeupbul and Deuknambul Buddha images.\n\n### Busan Tower in Yongdusan Park\n\nBuilt in 1973, Busan Tower is an iconic 120-metre-tall landmark with a small cafe and an observation deck offering stunning views. It is also home to 2 museums: the Museum of World Folk Instruments, and the Exhibition Hall of World Model Boats.\n\n### Gamcheon Culture Village\n\nGamcheon has long been home to the refugees who took shelter in Busan during the Korean War. It was formerly a shantytown that was revitalized through a city regeneration project. It features colorfully painted houses clinging to the hillside and has earned the nickname “Santorini in Korea”.\n\n### Busan Museum of Art\n\nThe Busan Museum of Art is must-visit if you want to have a look and feel of what Korean contemporary art is all about. The museum features 5 floors comprising exhibition halls, educational rooms and archives, while a sculpture park is located outside the building.\n\nThis article includes opinions of the Go Guides editorial team. Hotels.com compensates authors for their writing appearing on this site; such compensation may include travel and other costs.\n\n### Related stories\n\nPrevious\n\n[![Best Family Spots in         "summary": "### Busan Travel Essentials Summary\n\n**1. Best Time to Visit:**\n- **Climate:** Humid subtropical with average temperatures around 15°C year-round. \n- **Peak Season:** July-August. Ideal months for visitors are May-June and September-November due to pleasant weather.\n\n**2. Busan Basics:**\n- **Power:** Plug types C and F, 220 V – 60 Hz.\n- **Currency:** South Korean Won (₩).\n- **Emergency Numbers:** Fire/Medical - 119; Police - 112.\n\n**3. Getting to Busan:**\n- **Airport:** Gimhae International Airport (PUS); offers various transportation options like taxis, light rail transit, and buses.\n- **Ferries:** Regular ferries from international ports such as Osaka and Kyushu.\n- **Train Services:** Busan Station connects to the KTX service; multiple stations available.\n\n**4. Getting Around:**\n- **Public Transport:** Extensive bus and subway networks; taxis also available.\n- **Transportation Cards:** Cashbee and Mybi cards facilitate travel.\n\n**5. Main Annual Events:**\n- **Haeundae Sand Festival:** Late May.\n- **Busan International Rock Festival:** Late July.\n- **Busan International Film Festival:** October.\n\n**6. Key Landmarks:**\n- **Haedong Yonggungsa Temple:** Seaside temple famous for sculptures.\n- **Busan Tower:** Iconic observation deck in Yongdusan Park.\n- **Gamcheon Culture Village:** Known for colorful houses and rich history.\n- **Busan Museum of Art:** Home to contemporary art exhibitions."
      },

        "url": "https://www.visitbusan.net/en/index.do?menuCd=DOM_000000304011003000",
        "title": "Preparing for a Trip | Useful Information",
        "description": "# Busan Metro\n## Tickets\n- ![QR승차권](https://www.visitbusan.net/upload_data/board_data/BBS_0000014/171636446314756.png)\n\nQR Ticket (single use)\n\nAvailable for Adults, Teenagers, Multi-child families, Children, and Preferential Ticket holders\n\n- ![모바일승차권](https://www.visitbusan.net/upload_data/board_data/BBS_0000014/171636446315193.png)\n\nMobile Pass\n\nAvailable for Adults, Teenagers, and Children\n\n- ![정기승차권](https://www.visitbusan.net/upload_data/board_data/BBS_0000014/171636446315702.png)\n\nCommuter Pass\n\n(1-day, 3-day, 30-day)\n\n\\* Not valid on other transportation such as Donghae Line and Busan-Gimhae Light Rail Transit\n\n- ![교통카드](https://www.visitbusan.net/upload_data/board_data/BBS_0000014/171636446312329.png)\n\nPublic transit card\n\nAvailable for Adults, Teenagers, and Children\n\nPass options include QR tickets, mobile passes, commuter passes, and public transit cards.\n\nCommuter passes are available for 1-day, 3-day, and 30-day durations. QR tickets, mobile passes, and public transit cards have fares that vary based on distance, while commuter passes have fixed rates regardless of distance.\n\nSingle-use QR tickets can be purchased at ticket dispensing machines located within the stations. Mobile passes can be used by downloading the ‘Busan Subway' application from Google Play Store or Apple App Store.\n\n# From the Airport to Downtown Busan\nGimhae International Airport is where all the international and domestic flights for Busan arrive. It is located about an hour away from downtown Busan. There are many convenient ways to reach downtown Busan from the airport.\n\nThe fares vary depending on the bus type and your destination. | | Subway | Taking light rail transit at the airport and transferring at Sasang Station (Busan Metro Line 2) or Daejeo Station (Busan Metro Line 3) is the best and cheapest way to get to downtown Busan. | ※The actual travel duration and fare may be subject to change. |\n\n버스, 지하철, 택시로 공항에서 도심가는 방법을 설명한 표\n\n![방문자 통계](https://www.visitbusan.net/visitcounter/visitcounter.do?refer=DOM_0000003)",
        "position": 15,
        "markdown": "[본문 바로가기](https://www.visitbusan.net/en/index.do?menuCd=DOM_000000304011003000#cBody)\n\n#### Preparing for a Trip\n\n- [Currency exchange/Roaming/ZIM CARRY](https://www.visitbusan.net/en/index.do?menuCd=DOM_000000303012008000)\n- [Visa & Immigration](https://www.visitbusan.net/en/index.do?menuCd=DOM_000000304011002000)\n- [Transportation](https://www.visitbusan.net/en/index.do?menuCd=DOM_000000304011003000)\n- [Emergency Medical Services](https://www.visitbusan.net/en/index.do?menuCd=DOM_000000303012004000)\n- [City Guide for Muslim Travelers](https://www.visitbusan.net/en/index.do?menuCd=DOM_000000303012005000)\n- [Free interpretation mobile app](https://www.visitbusan.net/en/index.do?menuCd=DOM_000000303012006000)\n- [VISIT BUSAN PASS](https://www.visitbusan.net/en/index.do?menuCd=DOM_000000303012007000)\n\n# Transportation\n\n# Busan Metro\n\nThe Busan Metro consists of subway lines 1–4, the Busan-Gimhae Light Rail Transit (BGL), and the Donghae Line, connecting major city areas across Busan, Gimhae, and Yangsan in Gyeongsangnam-do.\n\n\nIt is a convenient way to reach your desired destination. It operates from 05:00 to 23:59. The schedule for the first and last trains varies depending on the subway station. The intervals also vary depending on the time zone. For more details, please refer to the website or the subway schedule inside the station.\n\n\n## Tickets\n\n- ![QR승차권](https://www.visitbusan.net/upload_data/board_data/BBS_0000014/171636446314756.png)\n\nQR Ticket (single use)\n\nAvailable for Adults, Teenagers, Multi-child families,\nChildren, and Preferential Ticket holders\n\n- ![모바일승차권](https://www.visitbusan.net/upload_data/board_data/BBS_0000014/171636446315193.png)\n\nMobile Pass\n\nAvailable for Adults, Teenagers, and Children\n\n- ![정기승차권](https://www.visitbusan.net/upload_data/board_data/BBS_0000014/171636446315702.png)\n\nCommuter Pass\n\n(1-day, 3-day, 30-day)\n\n\n\\\\* Not valid on other transportation such as Donghae Line and Busan-Gimhae Light Rail Transit\n\n- ![교통카드](https://www.visitbusan.net/upload_data/board_data/BBS_0000014/171636446312329.png)\n\nPublic transit card\n\nAvailable for Adults, Teenagers, and Children\n\n\nPass options include QR tickets, mobile passes, commuter passes, and public transit cards.\n\nCommuter passes are available for 1-day, 3-day, and 30-day durations. QR tickets, mobile passes, and public transit cards have fares that vary based on distance, while commuter passes have fixed rates regardless of distance.\n\nSingle-use QR tickets can be purchased at ticket dispensing machines located within the stations. Mobile passes can be used by downloading the ‘Busan Subway' application from Google Play Store or Apple App Store.\n\n\n- Busan Metro Overview [Shortcut](http://www.humetro.busan.kr/homepage/cyberstation/mapeng.do)\n- Busan-Gimhae Light Rail Transit Overview [Shortcut](http://www.bglrt.com/00211/00215.web)\n\n# Bus\n\nBusan’s buses often travel up and down the mountains due to the topographical features of the city with a number of mountains.\n\nHighly experienced bus drivers, for whom driving along narrow paths in an old town is a piece of cake, will help you get to your destination in a safe and fast way.\n\nThere are several bus types: regular bus, city bus with guaranteed seats, express city bus with guaranteed seats, and shuttle bus.\n\nYou can recognize the bus type by looking at the bus number. Regular buses have numbers ranging from 1 to 500, city bus in 58-1, 203, 221, or 3nn, regular express city bus in 1nnn, and non-stop express city bus in 2nnn. There is no separate night bus. Please note that an extra charge will occur once it is past 23:40.\n\n\nIn addition, the following guidelines apply to items you can bring on board.\n\n- \\- Maximum weight per person: under 20 kg\n- \\- Size limit: under 50×40×20 cm\n\n→ This means items like a 20-inch carry-on suitcase or a 40-liter shopping cart are generally acceptable.\n\nLarger suitcases or bulky items cannot be brought onto the bus. If your luggage exceeds the limit, it is recommended to use other transportation options such as the subway, airport limousine bus, or taxi.\n\n- Busan Bus Overview [Shortcut](https://its.busan.go.kr/)\n\n# Airport Limousine\n\nThere is a bus route connecting the Gimhae International Airport to Haeundae New Town. Airport Limousine is a fast way to reach Haeundae from the airport through the Dongseo Expressway and Hwangnyeongsan Tunnel. It has the same route from the Gimhae International Airport to the Haeundae Beach of Bus No. 307; nevertheless, it stops in Namcheon-dong, Gwangan-dong, and at Suyeong Junction. It takes about an hour, which is about the same when you use the Busan Metro. If you find it difficult to drag your luggage and transfer to another subway line, the Airport Limousine would be a better choice.\n\n\n- Airport Limousine Overview [Shortcut](https://www.airport.co.kr/gimhaeeng/cms/frCon/index.do?MENU_ID=110)\n\n# Transfer\n\nYou can get transfer discounts, limited to twice per travel, if you transfer within a time limit of 30 minutes to a different or the same mode of transportation (such as intracity bus, subway, and shuttle bus) with a different route. To get discounts, you must hold a transit pass and bring it into contact with a fare reader on your way out of an intracity bus or a subway station. As for the Busan Metro, transfer discounts are available only when you stay inside the subway platform. Please note that there is no transfer discount available for the Airport Limousine.\n\n\n# Transit Pass\n\nA transit pass is an electronic payment method used for transportation. You can also make a payment with it just like cash at any place such as a convenient store if the transit pass payment system is available. After purchasing a transit pass, you can add as much money as you want to your pass. Every time you use it, the amount comes off from your original balance. If you have a transit pass, you can also get transportation and transfer discounts.\n\nHanaro Card is the official transit pass used in Busan although you can also get transfer discounts by using other regional Mybi, T-money, and Cashbee cards including the Gimhae-for-you Card along with the deferred payment transit passes.\n\nYou can add money to your transit pass by using a ticket machine inside the Busan Metro stations and the railway stations along the Donghae Line or at convenience stores and Busan Bank branches.\n\n\n\n# From the Airport to Downtown Busan\n\nGimhae International Airport is where all the international and domestic flights for Busan arrive. It is located about an hour away from downtown Busan. There are many convenient ways to reach downtown Busan from the airport.\n\n\n| Bus | You can take an intracity bus, airport limousine, express intracity bus, shuttle bus, and intracity bus. The fares vary depending on the bus type and your destination. |\n| Subway | Taking light rail transit at the airport and transferring at Sasang Station (Busan Metro Line 2) or Daejeo Station (Busan Metro Line 3) is the best and cheapest way to get to downtown Busan. |\n| Taxi | 시Busan has three major taxi types: regular, deluxe, and large taxis. Although deluxe and large taxis costs 50% more compared to a regular taxi, they are larger in size and more luxurious. ※The actual travel duration and fare may be subject to change. |\n\n버스, 지하철, 택시로 공항에서 도심가는 방법을 설명한 표\n\n![방문자 통계](https://www.visitbusan.net/visitcounter/visitcounter.do?refer=DOM_0000003)",

Transfer discounts are available within a 30-minute window between transportation modes. Lastly, information on the transit pass system, including the Hanaro Card and payment methods, is provided."
